Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

40: +// TestProcessNetworkModeLabNamedAsNode covers a node whose name is also the lab name. The container
41: +// it references must be looked up under the full lab prefix.
42: +func TestProcessNetworkModeLabNamedAsNode(t *testing.T) {
43: +	const holder = "clab-srl02-srl02-netns"
44: +	id := strings.Repeat("a", 64)
45: +
46: +	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
47: +		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
48: +
49: +		switch {
50: +		case strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/containers/json"):
51: +			if !strings.Contains(r.URL.Query().Get("filters"), "^"+holder+"$") {
52: +				_, _ = w.Write([]byte(`[]`))
53: +				return
54: +			}
55: +			_, _ = w.Write([]byte(
56: +				`[{"Id":"` + id + `","Names":["/` + holder +
57: +					`"],"NetworkSettings":{"Networks":{}}}]`,
58: +			))
59: +		case strings.Contains(r.URL.Path, id):
60: +			_, _ = w.Write([]byte(`{"Id":"` + id + `","State":{"Pid":1234}}`))
61: +		default:
62: +			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNotFound)
63: +		}
64: +	}))
65: +	defer srv.Close()
66: +
67: +	cli, err := dockerC.NewClientWithOpts(
68: +		dockerC.WithHost(srv.URL),
69: +		dockerC.WithVersion("1.43"),
70: +	)
71: +	if err != nil {
72: +		t.Fatal(err)
73: +	}
74: +	defer cli.Close()
75: +

79: +		config: clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{Timeout: defaultTimeout},

93: +	)

96: +	}
97: +

99: +		t.Fatalf("NetworkMode = %q, want %q", hostConfig.NetworkMode, want)
100: +	}
101: +}

170: +func TestLabContainerName(t *testing.T) {
171: +	t.Parallel()
172: +
173: +	tests := map[string]struct {
174: +		longName  string
175: +		shortName string
176: +		want      string
177: +	}{
178: +		"default prefix":    {"clab-lab-n1", "n1", "clab-lab-n1-netns"},
179: +		"lab named as node": {"clab-srl02-srl02", "srl02", "clab-srl02-srl02-netns"},
180: +		"lab name contains node name": {
181: +			"clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1",
182: +			"leaf1",
183: +			"clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1-netns",
184: +		},
185: +		"node name in prefix": {"lab-x-lab", "lab", "lab-x-lab-netns"},
186: +		"lab name prefix":     {"lab-n1", "n1", "lab-n1-netns"},
187: +		"empty prefix":        {"n1", "n1", "n1-netns"},
188: +	}
189: +
190: +	for name, tt := range tests {
191: +		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
192: +			t.Parallel()
193: +
194: +			n := &NodeConfig{LongName: tt.longName, ShortName: tt.shortName}
195: +			if got := n.LabContainerName(tt.shortName + "-netns"); got != tt.want {
196: +				t.Fatalf("LabContainerName() = %q, want %q", got, tt.want)
197: +			}
198: +		})
199: +	}
200: +}

213: +func (n *NodeConfig) LabContainerName(shortName string) string {

Reasoning
Let me understand the change:

1. New helper `NodeConfig.LabContainerName(shortName string)`: returns `strings.TrimSuffix(n.LongName, n.ShortName) + shortName`.
2. Used in `runtime/docker/docker.go` `processNetworkMode` replacing `strings.SplitN(node.LongName, node.ShortName, 2)[0] + netMode[1]`.
3. Used in `runtime/podman/util.go` `createContainerSpec`.
4. Tests added.

Key correctness analysis: The old code `strings.SplitN(LongName, ShortName, 2)[0] + netMode[1]` — for LongName "clab-srl02-srl02", ShortName "srl02", SplitN finds the first occurrence of "srl02" in LongName... Actually SplitN splits on separator occurrences. "clab-srl02-srl02".SplitN("srl02", 2) → ["clab-", "srl02"]. prefix = "clab-". So old code produced "clab-" + "srl02-netns" = "clab-srl02-srl02-netns" — correct! Wait, actually the old code was correct in this case. Hmm.

But what if the lab name contains the short name? E.g. lab name "dc1-leaf1" and node "leaf1": LongName "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1", ShortName "leaf1". SplitN finds first occurrence: "clab-dc1-" ... wait "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1".SplitN("leaf1", 2) → ["clab-dc1-", "-leaf1"]. prefix = "clab-dc1-". Result: "clab-dc1-leaf1-netns" — WRONG, because the actual node's container is named "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1"... wait no. Actually the container name of node "leaf1" in lab "dc1-leaf1" is "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1". The referenced netMode is "container:leaf1-netns" where leaf1-netns is another node. Its long name is "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1-netns". Old code gives "clab-dc1-" + "leaf1-netns" = "clab-dc1-leaf1-netns" — wrong. New code: TrimSuffix("clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1", "leaf1") = "clab-dc1-leaf1-" → "clab-dc1-leaf1-" + "leaf1-netns" = "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1-netns" — correct.

So the new helper fixes the case where the lab name contains the node name (the referenced container's shortname). The docstring says "Only the end of LongName is the short name, as the lab name can contain the same text."

Now edge cases of TrimSuffix:
- If LongName doesn't end with ShortName? TrimSuffix returns unchanged; then result = LongName + shortName. When could LongName not end with ShortName? For external containers? In the docker path, node.LongName for the node being processed... Let me check how LongName is set. Typically LongName = prefix + ShortName where prefix = "clab-" + labName + "-". So LongName always ends with ShortName. But for ext-container kind, ShortName may be the external container name and LongName may be empty? Let me check.

Also: what if ShortName is empty? TrimSuffix(LongName, "") returns LongName; result = LongName + shortName. Old code SplitN(LongName, "", 2) — SplitN with empty separator splits into... Actually strings.SplitN(s, "", 2) splits into UTF-8 sequences: ["f","irst..."]? SplitN with empty sep splits after each UTF-8 sequence; N=2 gives [first char, rest]. So old code with empty ShortName would give first-char prefix. But empty ShortName is unlikely at this point.

Now, another consideration: the docker processNetworkMode old code: `contName := strings.SplitN(node.LongName, node.ShortName, 2)[0] + netMode[1]`. Hmm, wait, there's a subtlety: what if the netMode-referenced container is NOT in the same lab (external container)? Then GetContainer fails and it falls back to using netMode[1] directly presumably. Let me check the surrounding code.

Also consider: what if the referenced container's short name differs from the current node's short name but the current node's LongName ends with... The helper uses n.ShortName (the current node's shortname) to strip the suffix from n.LongName, then appends the referenced shortName. That's the correct approach since LongName = labPrefix + ShortName.

Wait — is that right? LongName = "clab-<lab>-<nodename>". TrimSuffix(LongName, ShortName) = "clab-<lab>-". Then + referenced short name. Correct as long as the referenced container is in the same lab. If referenced container is external (different lab), the produced name is wrong but the old code had the same issue (prefix from own lab) — and then GetContainer fails and falls back to external name. Let me check the fallback in docker.go.

Also check: does TrimSuffix handle the case where ShortName is a suffix of the lab name but node name differs? E.g., lab "srl02", node "srl02-x"? LongName "clab-srl02-srl02-x", ShortName "srl02-x". TrimSuffix strips "srl02-x" → "clab-srl02-". Fine.

What about the old code behavior when node name appears in the lab name earlier: e.g., lab "x-srl02", node "srl02": LongName "clab-x-srl02-srl02", ShortName "srl02". Old: SplitN first occurrence → ["clab-x-", "srl02"] prefix "clab-x-". Result "clab-x-srl02-netns" — WRONG (should be clab-x-srl02-srl02-netns). New: TrimSuffix → "clab-x-srl02-" + "srl02-netns" = "clab-x-srl02-srl02-netns" — correct. Good, new code fixes this.

Hmm wait, but wait. What about the case where the referenced node name is a prefix of the current node's name? E.g., current node "srl02", referenced "srl02-netns"? Not relevant.

One more subtle issue: the helper's parameter is `shortName`, and callers pass `netMode[1]` which is the referenced container name (may include netns suffix or a fully qualified name). Fine.

Now — potential edge case: node.ShortName empty. When is processNetworkMode called? During container creation for each node. ShortName should be set. But what about ext-container nodes? Let's check whether processNetworkMode can be called with a node whose LongName doesn't end with ShortName. For ext-container kind, LongName is empty? Let me check types. Actually let me look at the code.

Also, another subtle: TrimSuffix strips only one occurrence of the suffix — good. But what if LongName == ShortName (empty prefix, e.g., lab name empty)? Test case "empty prefix": {"n1","n1","n1-netns"} → TrimSuffix("n1","n1")="" + "n1-netns" = "n1-netns". Old: SplitN("n1","n1",2)[0]="" same. OK.

Now, potential issue: TrimSuffix vs the actual semantics. The old code took the prefix BEFORE THE FIRST occurrence of shortName. The new takes everything before the LAST occurrence. In the standard case LongName = prefix + ShortName with ShortName occurring once at the end; but if ShortName occurs in the lab name too, they differ. The new is correct because LongName is defined as prefix+ShortName.

1856: func (d *DockerRuntime) processNetworkMode(
1857: 	ctx context.Context,
1858: 	containerNetworkingConfig *networkapi.NetworkingConfig,
1859: 	containerHostConfig *container.HostConfig,
1860: 	containerConfig *container.Config,
1861: 	node *clabtypes.NodeConfig,
1862: ) error {
1863: 	netMode := strings.SplitN(node.NetworkMode, ":", 2)
1864: 
1865: 	switch netMode[0] {
1866: 	case "none":
1867: 		containerHostConfig.NetworkMode = "none"
1868: 	// clab allows its containers to be attached to a netns of another container
1869: 	// this can be a container that is managed by clab, or an external container.
1870: 	case "container":
1871: 		// We expect exactly two arguments in this case ("container" keyword & cont. name/ID)
1872: 		if len(netMode) != 2 {
1873: 			return fmt.Errorf(
1874: 				"container network mode was specified for container %q, but we failed to parse the network-mode instruction: %q",
1875: 				node.ShortName,
1876: 				netMode,
1877: 			)
1878: 		}
1879: 
1880: 		// also cont. ID shouldn't be empty
1881: 		if netMode[1] == "" {
1882: 			return fmt.Errorf(
1883: 				"container network mode was specified for container %q, referenced container name appears to be empty: %q",
1884: 				node.ShortName,
1885: 				netMode,
1886: 			)
1887: 		}
1888: 
1889: 		// to support using network stack of containers that are scheduled by clab
1890: 		// or containers deployed externally (i.e. by k8s kind)
1891: 		// we first check if container name referenced in network-mode node property exists
1892: 		// internally, aka within clab topology. If it doesn't, we assume that users referred to an
1893: 		// external container. extract lab/topo prefix to craft a full container name if an internal
1894: 		// container is referenced.
1895: 		contName := node.LabContainerName(netMode[1])
1896: 
1897: 		_, err := d.GetContainer(ctx, contName)
1898: 		if err != nil {
1899: 			log.Debugf(
1900: 				"container %q was not found by its name, assuming it is exists externally with unprefixed",
1901: 				contName,
1902: 			)
1903: 
1904: 			// container doesn't exist internally, so we assume it exists externally
1905: 			// with a non-prefixed name.
1906: 			contName = netMode[1]
1907: 
1908: 			if _, err := d.GetContainer(ctx, contName); err != nil {
1909: 				return fmt.Errorf(
1910: 					"container %q is referenced in network-mode, but was not found",
1911: 					netMode[1],
1912: 				)
1913: 			}
1914: 		}
1915: 
1916: 		// compile the net spec
1917: 		containerHostConfig.NetworkMode = container.NetworkMode("container:" + contName)
1918: 
1919: 		// unset the hostname as it is not supported in this case
1920: 		containerConfig.Hostname = ""
1921: 	case "host":
1922: 		containerHostConfig.NetworkMode = "host"
1923: 	default:
1924: 		containerHostConfig.NetworkMode = container.NetworkMode(d.mgmt.Network)
1925: 
1926: 		containerNetworkingConfig.EndpointsConfig = map[string]*networkapi.EndpointSettings{
1927: 			d.mgmt.Network: {
1928: 				IPAMConfig: &networkapi.EndpointIPAMConfig{
1929: 					IPv4Address: node.MgmtIPv4Address,
1930: 					IPv6Address: node.MgmtIPv6Address,
1931: 				},
1932: 				Aliases:    node.Aliases,
1933: 				MacAddress: node.MacAddress,
1934: 			},
1935: 		}
1936: 	}
1937: 
1938: 	return nil
1939: }
1940: 
1941: // GetContainerStatus retrieves the ContainerStatus of the named container.
1942: func (d *DockerRuntime) GetContainerStatus(
1943: 	ctx context.Context,
1944: 	cID string,
1945: ) clabruntime.ContainerStatus {
1946: 	inspect, err := d.Client.ContainerInspect(ctx, cID)
1947: 	if err != nil {
1948: 		return clabruntime.NotFound
1949: 	}
1950: 	switch inspect.State.Status {
1951: 	case "running":
1952: 		return clabruntime.Running
1953: 	case "paused":
1954: 		return clabruntime.Paused
1955: 	case "created":
1956: 		return clabruntime.Created
1957: 	case "restarting":
1958: 		return clabruntime.Restarting
1959: 	case "removing":
1960: 		return clabruntime.Removing
1961: 	case "exited", "dead":
1962: 		return clabruntime.Stopped
1963: 	default:
1964: 		return clabruntime.NotFound
1965: 	}
1966: }
1967: 
1968: // containerPid returns the pid of a container by its ID using inspect.
1969: func (d *DockerRuntime) containerPid(ctx context.Context, cID string) (int, error) {
1970: 	inspect, err := d.Client.ContainerInspect(ctx, cID)
1971: 	if err != nil {
1972: 		return 0, fmt.Errorf("container %q cannot be found: %w", cID, err)
1973: 	}
1974: 	return inspect.State.Pid, nil
1975: }
1976: 
1977: // IsHealthy returns true is the container is reported as being healthy, false otherwise.
1978: func (d *DockerRuntime) IsHealthy(ctx context.Context, cID string) (bool, error) {
1979: 	inspect, err := d.Client.ContainerInspect(ctx, cID)
1980: 	if err != nil {
1981: 		return false, err
1982: 	}
1983: 	// catch no healthchecks defined
1984: 	if inspect.State.Health == nil {
1985: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("no health information available for container: %s", cID)
1986: 	}
1987: 	return inspect.State.Health.Status == "healthy", nil
1988: }

1992: 		Stdin:  true,
1993: 		Stream: true,
1994: 		Stdout: true,
1995: 		Stderr: true,
1996: 	})
1997: 	if err != nil {
1998: 		return err

Now check `nodes/ext_container/ext_container.go`: `s.Cfg.LongName = s.Cfg.ShortName`. So for ext-containers, LongName == ShortName. In that case, `LabContainerName(x)` = TrimSuffix(ShortName, ShortName) + x = x. That's the unprefixed name — which is correct behavior for ext-containers (they aren't prefixed). Actually wait — the podman `createContainerSpec` unconditionally prefixes for `container:` network mode. For an ext-container node with `network-mode: container:foo`, the old code: SplitN("somecont", "somecont", 2)[0] = "" → contName = "foo" (unprefixed). New: TrimSuffix("somecont","somecont") = "" → "foo". Same. Good.

25: 	t.Parallel()
26: 	ctrl := gomock.NewController(t)
27: 	c := &CLab{Nodes: map[string]clabnodes.Node{}}
28: 	const count = 256

217: // newNetworkModePlanTestLab models a new link on a running target and a chain
218: // of existing namespace-sharing dependents. Interface discovery is mocked so
219: // the complete planner can run without creating real network namespaces.
220: func newNetworkModePlanTestLab(
221: 	t *testing.T,
222: 	dependentStatus clabruntime.ContainerStatus,
223: 	mode clabnodes.LinkApplyMode,
224: ) (*CLab, map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup) {
225: 	t.Helper()
226: 	ctrl := gomock.NewController(t)
227: 	paths := &clabtypes.TopoPaths{}
228: 	if err := paths.SetLabDir(t.TempDir()); err != nil {
229: 		t.Fatal(err)
230: 	}
231: 	c := &CLab{
232: 		Config:    &Config{Topology: clabtypes.NewTopology()},
233: 		TopoPaths: paths,
234: 		Nodes:     map[string]clabnodes.Node{},
235: 		Links:     map[int]clablinks.Link{},
236: 	}
237: 	current := map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup{}
238: 	for name, target := range map[string]string{"target": "", "sidecar": "target", "leaf": "sidecar"} {
239: 		cfg := &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: name, LongName: "clab-netmode-test-" + name}
240: 		status := clabruntime.Running
241: 		if target != "" {
242: 			cfg.NetworkMode = "container:" + target
243: 			status = dependentStatus
244: 		}
245: 		node := clabmocksmocknodes.NewMockNode(ctrl)
246: 		node.EXPECT().Config().Return(cfg).AnyTimes()
247: 		node.EXPECT().GetShortName().Return(name).AnyTimes()
248: 		node.EXPECT().GetContainerStatus(gomock.Any()).Return(status).AnyTimes()

Reasoning
There's `nodeLongName(target)` in `core/config.go` used for PidMode resolution. Interesting — `resolvePidMode` also does prefixing of `container:` references. It uses `c.nodeLongName(target)` which is the correct lookup approach there (looking up the target in topology nodes). That's fine.

Now let me check `nodeLongName`:
```go
func (c *CLab) nodeLongName(nodeName string) string {
	switch *c.Config.Prefix {
	case "":
		return nodeName
	case "__lab-name":
		return fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s", c.Config.Name, nodeName)
	default:
		return fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s-%s", *c.Config.Prefix, c.Config.Name, nodeName)
	}
}
```

So LongName = prefix + "-" + labName + "-" + nodeName. Note: with prefix "clab" → "clab-<lab>-<node>". With `__lab-name` prefix → "<lab>-<node>".

Now the helper: TrimSuffix(LongName, ShortName). If LongName = "<lab>-<node>" (prefix __lab-name) and lab name contains node name at the end: e.g., lab "x-lab", node "lab": LongName "x-lab-lab", ShortName "lab" → TrimSuffix → "x-lab-" + "lab-netns" → "x-lab-lab-netns". Correct.

What about the case where the node name is such that LongName does NOT end with ShortName? For sros components: ShortName = calcComponentName(ShortName, slot) = ShortName + "-lc" + slot? Let me check calcComponentName at line 832. And the component's LongName = calcComponentName(LongName, slot) = LongName + suffix. So LongName ends with the component ShortName. OK.

For the root node? Let's check where a root node config is created. `RootNodeLongName = "clab-root-node-longname"` is a label. There's likely a node with ShortName "root"? Let me search. Actually there might be a special "mgmt-net" root node for host networking in sros/srl netns handling — e.g. `nodes/srl/netns.go` creates a config with LongName: longName. Let me check that quickly. Actually netns.go — creates a container for the netns dummy node. Let me check its ShortName/LongName.

Let me read nodes/srl/netns.go and nodes/sros/netns.go briefly.

Reasoning
OK, so the netns holder node has LongName = parent LongName + "-netns", ShortName = parent ShortName + "-netns" — so LongName ends with ShortName. That's exactly the "lab named as node" scenario in the docker test: node srl02's holder is "clab-srl02-srl02-netns"; when the srl container (shortName srl02, LongName clab-srl02-srl02) uses network-mode "container:srl02-netns", the old code: SplitN("clab-srl02-srl02","srl02",2) → ["clab-", "srl02"] → prefix "clab-" → "clab-srl02-netns" — WRONG (should be "clab-srl02-srl02-netns"). So this PR fixes a real bug: when the lab name equals/contains the node name, the netns holder reference is mis-resolved. Good — the fix has genuine motivation.

Wait, actually let me double check SplitN semantics: strings.SplitN("clab-srl02-srl02", "srl02", 2) — SplitN splits at the first occurrence of the separator: result ["clab-", "-srl02"]? Let's compute: the string is "clab-srl02-srl02". First occurrence of "srl02" is at index 5. So split into "clab-" and "-srl02" (n=2, so remainder is the second element). [0] = "clab-". So contName = "clab-" + "srl02-netns" = "clab-srl02-netns". The actual holder is "clab-srl02-srl02-netns". So GetContainer fails, then falls back to external "srl02-netns" which doesn't exist → error "container srl02-netns is referenced in network-mode, but was not found". So yes, a real bug fixed.

Now the new helper: TrimSuffix("clab-srl02-srl02", "srl02") = "clab-srl02-" → "clab-srl02-srl02-netns". Correct.

Edge: what if LongName does not end with ShortName? Cases:
- ext_container: LongName == ShortName → TrimSuffix gives "" → returns just shortName param. Same as before.
- Any node whose LongName is modified without ShortName? Let me check other places LongName is assigned/modified. E.g., tailscale proxy: LongName: longName. Let me check a few: `cmd/tools_sshx.go` LongName: name (no ShortName set at all — but those NodeConfigs are only used for lookup, not for processNetworkMode).

Are there kinds that mutate Cfg.LongName after init such that it doesn't end with ShortName? Let me grep for `.LongName =` more broadly (already did: ext_container, sros calcComponentName, baseLongName). sros: ShortName and LongName both get the suffix appended, consistent.

What about `GetContainerName()`? Let me check: there may be a method GetContainerName on NodeConfig. Let me look. Actually types/types.go probably has GetContainerName returning LongName or ShortName based on... Let me check. Also check whether there are other places that construct "prefix + netMode[1]" style lookups, e.g., ipam or the api server. Let me grep for `netMode[1]` and `NetworkMode` usages.

Also verify the docker test compiles: uses `defaultTimeout` — check it exists in runtime/docker package. grep.

1550: // Transform docker-specific to generic container format.
1551: func (d *DockerRuntime) produceGenericContainerList(
1552: 	ctx context.Context,

Good. `i.ID[:12]` fine. NetworkSettings.Networks = {} empty, bridgeName "clab", `i.NetworkSettings.Networks["clab"]` not ok → len(Networks)==0, so skip; bridgeName != "" → set... let me read the rest briefly — probably sets MgmtIPv4 etc from network inspect. Fine.

Also note the mock handles the case where the filter doesn't match → returns `[]` — GetContainer returns error "found unexpected number of containers: 0"? Wait, the flow: processNetworkMode first tries contName = LabContainerName("srl02-netns") = "clab-srl02-srl02-netns" → matches holder → returns the container. Good. The test's "empty result" branch covers the fallback path? Actually the fallback would happen only if the first lookup fails; the test only exercises the success path. That's fine for the regression.

Wait — one check: does the mock's `/containers/json` branch also get hit for the fallback call `GetContainer(ctx, "srl02-netns")`? Only if the first call failed. In this test the first call succeeds. Good.

Hmm, one subtlety: in the first GetContainer call, the filter regex is `^clab-srl02-srl02-netns$`. The mock checks `strings.Contains(r.URL.Query().Get("filters"), "^"+holder+"$")`. Good.

Now, is `i.ID[:12]` — ID is 64 'a's, fine.

Now the podman test. Let me check the podman test file around the new test, and `createContainerSpec` requirements — the new test creates `PodmanRuntime{mgmt: &types.MgmtNet{Network: "clab"}}` with cfg Image, Labels, NetworkMode. Does createContainerSpec need more fields (e.g., cfg.LongName used for logging, cfg.PortBindings nil ok)? It already passes given other tests in the same file use similar minimal configs (e.g., TestCreateContainerSpecPreservesImageCommandDefaults). Fine.

Now the key remaining question — edge cases in `LabContainerName`:

1. What if the referenced container name (netMode[1]) is itself a full container name or an ID? E.g., `network-mode: container:clab-otherlab-node1` referencing a container in another lab. Old behavior: prefix + "clab-otherlab-node1" → GetContainer fails → fallback to "clab-otherlab-node1" (external). New behavior: same fallback. Fine.

2. What if the referenced name happens to make LabContainerName produce a name that exists but is the wrong container? E.g., lab "dc1", node "leaf1" (LongName "clab-dc1-leaf1"), network-mode references "container:leaf1". LabContainerName → "clab-dc1-leaf1" → that's the node itself! Old code: SplitN("clab-dc1-leaf1","leaf1",2)[0] = "clab-dc1-" → "clab-dc1-leaf1" — same result. Both would self-reference; docker would reject sharing netns with self at runtime. Not a regression.

Hmm wait, actually there IS a subtle difference between old and new for a specific case: what if ShortName is not a suffix of LongName? Old code: SplitN returns [LongName, ""] → [0] = LongName → result LongName + netMode[1]. New: TrimSuffix returns LongName unchanged → same. Identical.

3. What if ShortName is empty and LongName nonempty? Old: SplitN(s, "", 2) → [s[:1], s[1:]] → prefix = first char. New: TrimSuffix(s, "") = s → result LongName + shortName. Different, but both weird; ShortName empty doesn't occur in practice for nodes being deployed. Not worth reporting maybe. Actually could there be a case where processNetworkMode is called with empty ShortName? No — createNodeCfg always sets ShortName.

Now, one more consideration: the semantics of "Only the end of LongName is the short name, as the lab name can contain the same text." — TrimSuffix strips n.ShortName from the end of n.LongName. But what if n.LongName == n.ShortName (ext-container) — returns shortName param, i.e., unprefixed. For the docker path, that means: for an ext-container node with network-mode container:X, contName = "X", GetContainer fails (probably), fallback to "X". Fine.

Now, is there any place where this helper should also be used but isn't? The `resolvePidMode` uses topology lookup — different mechanism, fine.

Now check the podman diff context more: the removed comment "Extract lab/topo prefix to provide a full (long) container name. Hackish way." was removed. Good.

Also check: in podman, unlike docker, there's no external fallback — if the referenced container is external, `cfg.LabContainerName(netMode[1])` produces prefixed name, which won't exist. But that was pre-existing behavior (old code also prefixed). Not a regression.

Now check documentation: is `network-mode` documented anywhere that the old behavior mattered? The change is a bug fix; docs for network-mode in docs/manual/nodes.md may say that container references need full names or not. Let me check if docs mention the naming behavior for network-mode. Also release notes: docs/rn/0.8x? The head is after 0.82. There's a release-note-review.mdc rule. Hmm, that rule file exists in the repo but is not part of the supplied standards.json (only karpathy and ponytail). standards.json lists only the two rules. So I apply those.

<content>
680: The `network-mode` configuration option set to `host` will launch the node in the [host networking mode](https://docs.docker.com/network/host/).
681: 
682: ```yaml
683: # example node definition with host networking mode
684: my-node:
685:   image: alpine:3
686:   network-mode: host
687: ```
688: 
689: #### container mode
690: 
691: Additionally, a node can join network namespace of another container - by referencing the node in the format of `container:parent_node_name`[^2]:
692: 
693: ```yaml
694: # example node definition with shared network namespace
695: my-node:
696:   kind: linux
697: sidecar-node:
698:   kind: linux
699:   network-mode: container:my-node # (1)
700:   startup-delay: 10 # (2)
701: ```
702: 
703: 1. `my-node` portion of a `network-mode` property instructs `sidecar-node` to join the network namespace of a `my-node`.
704: 2. `startup-delay` is required in this case in order to properly initialize the namespace of a parent container.
705: 
706: Container name used after `container:` portion can refer to a node defined in containerlab topology or can refer to a name of a container that was launched outside of containerlab. This is useful when containerlab node needs to connect to a network namespace of a container deployed by 3rd party management tool (e.g. k8s kind).
707: 
708: #### none mode
709: 
710: If you want to completely disable the networking stack on a container, you can use the `none` network mode. In this mode containerlab will deploy nodes without `eth0` interface and docker networking. See [docker docs](https://docs.docker.com/network/none/) for more details.
711: 
712: ### runtime
713: 
714: /// warning | Deprecated
715: The per-node `runtime` parameter is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Mixing runtimes in one lab is not supported: the management network is created, and the lab is destroyed and inspected, with the lab-wide runtime only. Set the runtime for the whole lab with the global `--runtime` flag or the `CLAB_RUNTIME` environment variable instead.
716: ///
717: 
718: By default containerlab nodes will be started by the `docker` container runtime. Besides that, containerlab has experimental support for the `podman` runtime.
719: 
720: The runtime is selected for the whole lab with the global `--runtime` flag or the `CLAB_RUNTIME` environment variable:
721: 
722: ```bash
723: containerlab deploy -t mylab.clab.yml --runtime podman
724: ```
725: 
726: /// note | Clabernetes lab runtime
727: The global `--runtime` flag and `CLAB_RUNTIME` environment variable accept
728: `c9s`. This is a whole-lab runtime that sends the topology to kubernetes as a
729: Clabernetes `Topology` resource.

(Showing lines 680-729 of 1199. Use offset=730 to continue.)
</content>

Reasoning
The docs describe referencing by node name — no change needed in the docs since the fix restores the documented behavior. The doc at line 706 says the reference "can refer to a node defined in containerlab topology or ... a container launched outside of containerlab". The fix makes the internal resolution correct for lab names containing node names. No doc drift.

Release notes: the latest is 0.82. Is there a convention that bug fixes get a release note? The `release-note-review.mdc` rule exists in the repo but wasn't listed in standards.json, so not a supplied rule. I could mention it as an optional note — but the docs check says "check that added, changed, deprecated, or removed user-visible behavior is reflected in relevant docs". The changed behavior is a bug fix restoring documented behavior; arguably there is no doc staleness. Bug fix entries in rn files are common (0.47.md references fixes). The 0.82 rn is presumably the current release. If the fix will land in 0.83, no rn file exists yet — maintainers typically add one at release. I'll not flag this, or flag as P4 optional? Hmm. The `release-note-review.mdc` in-repo rule suggests the project has a process, but it's not one of the supplied standards. I'll skip it or put it in coverage notes.

Now let me double-check the podman util.go around the change — did removing the `prefix` variable leave any other use of `prefix` in that function? The diff removed the only use. Let me grep `prefix :=` in util.go head to be sure. Also check `strings` import still used in podman util.go (yes, SplitN at line 204).

Also check the base versions to ensure only these hunks changed (docker.go might have other changes not shown? The diff only shows one hunk per file, plus the test additions). The manifest lists 6 changed paths; diff.txt covers all.

Let me verify diff.txt is complete — it ended at line 221 with types/types.go hunk. It seems complete.

Now potential issues to consider reporting:

1. `LabContainerName` doc comment: "returns the long container name of a node in the same lab as n. Only the end of LongName is the short name, as the lab name can contain the same text." This is accurate given LongName = prefix-labName-nodeName. But there's an edge: TrimSuffix strips the suffix unconditionally, even when LongName doesn't end with ShortName — in that case the result is LongName+shortName, which is the LongName of... nothing meaningful. But as analyzed, all NodeConfigs reaching these call sites have LongName ending in ShortName, except ext-containers where LongName == ShortName and the result correctly degrades to the unprefixed referenced name (matching old behavior). OK.

Wait, actually there's one more interesting case for ext-containers: ext-container LongName == ShortName. Old behavior: SplitN(ShortName, ShortName, 2)[0] = "" → unprefixed. New: TrimSuffix(ShortName, ShortName) = "" → unprefixed. Identical. Good.

2. What about the `srl-netns` holder node itself being referenced? Not relevant.

3. A subtle difference: old code split on the FIRST occurrence; new strips the LAST occurrence (from the end). Consider lab "srl02" node "srl02": covered. Consider lab "x", node "x-x"? ShortName "x-x", LongName "clab-x-x-x". Old: SplitN("clab-x-x-x","x-x",2) → first occurrence at index 5: ["clab-", "-x"] → "clab-" + netMode[1]. If referencing "x-x-netns" → "clab-x-x-netns" (wrong). New: TrimSuffix → "clab-x-" + "x-x-netns" = "clab-x-x-x-netns" (correct). Good.

4. Is there a test for the podman "external container" case? Not needed.

5. Performance: negligible.

6. Any concurrency issue: no.

7. The docker test constructs DockerRuntime with unexported fields — fine since same package.

8. Test quality: The docker test name says "LabNamedAsNode". Good.

9. Check `git-mutations.mdc` rule — not in standards.json, skip.

10. One more thing to verify: does `types/types.go` import `strings`? Yes — it's used elsewhere (line 347 strings.HasPrefix) and line 336. Confirmed by the head file already using strings at 347. Was `strings` already imported before the change? In base, line 347 also existed, so yes.

11. Check schema: no topology API change — internal only. No schema edit needed.

12. Coverage: no unit test for the docker fallback path (external container) — pre-existing. Also no test for podman external. Fine.

13. Potential issue: the docker test's fake server responds to `/containers/json` for any request whose path has that suffix; the second `GetContainer` (fallback) never fires. Fine.

14. Another potential concern: `LabContainerName` takes `shortName` param and appends — the caller passes `netMode[1]` which might be a container ID or a full name. The doc says "returns the long container name of a node in the same lab as n". Slight naming nuance: param is called shortName but caller may pass arbitrary names. Minor; not worth a finding? It's arguably fine.

15. Now think about whether there's any case where the new behavior could regress the previously-working old behavior. Old: SplitN(LongName, ShortName, 2)[0] — everything before the first occurrence of ShortName in LongName. New: everything before the last occurrence (i.e., LongName minus trailing ShortName). These differ only when ShortName occurs in LongName before the final position. When does that happen? LongName = P + "-" + lab + "-" + node (or lab-node with __lab-name prefix). Old is "correct" (produces prefix + referenced) only when the first occurrence is the trailing one — i.e., node name doesn't appear in lab name/prefix. When node name appears earlier in LongName, old produced a truncated prefix — wrong for the internal reference (GetContainer would fail and fall back to unprefixed, which then also fails for internal refs → error). But could old produce a name that coincidentally matched some OTHER container? E.g., lab "leaf1", node "leaf1" (LongName "clab-leaf1-leaf1"), referencing "container:leaf1-netns": old → "clab-leaf1-netns". If there were another node in the lab named... the netns holder is "clab-leaf1-leaf1-netns". "clab-leaf1-netns" doesn't exist unless a node literally named "leaf1-netns"... hmm, actually a node named "leaf1-netns"? Not typical since "-netns" is an internal suffix. Could old behavior have matched a container whose name is prefix+ref where prefix was truncated? For a node named e.g. "srl02" in lab "srl02", old gives "clab-srl02-netns" — no node produces that name. But consider lab "a", node "b", referencing "container:b": LongName "clab-a-b", ShortName "b" → old: SplitN → ["clab-a-", ""] wait "clab-a-b" first occurrence of "b" is at index 6 → [0]="clab-a-" → "clab-a-b" = the node itself (self-reference). New: TrimSuffix("clab-a-b","b") = "clab-a-" → "clab-a-b" same. OK.

Tricky: node "ab" in lab "a": LongName "clab-a-ab", ShortName "ab". Old: first occurrence of "ab" — is it at index 6? "clab-a-ab": indices: c0 l1 a2 b3 -4 a5 -6 a7 b8. Substring "ab" occurs at index 2 ("clab")! So SplitN first occurrence at index 2 → [0] = "cl" → contName = "cl" + ref. That's broken old behavior; new: TrimSuffix → "clab-a-" + ref → correct. So the new code fixes more cases where ShortName appears within "clab" prefix too.

Is there any case where old was right and new is wrong? New assumes LongName always ends with ShortName. If a node's LongName were set to something not ending in ShortName, new produces LongName+ref, old produced prefix-before-first-occurrence+ref. Both would be nonsense. So no regression.

16. Now, the one thing I want to double-check: in the docker path, after GetContainer succeeds, `containerHostConfig.NetworkMode = container.NetworkMode("container:" + contName)`. Docker's network mode "container:<name>" accepts container name — yes.

17. Check the podman side: `specgen.Namespace{NSMode: "container", Value: cfg.LabContainerName(netMode[1])}`. Fine.

18. Check whether there is a similar netns holder reference path for podman (srl netns) — same mechanism.

19. `types/node_config_test.go`: tests table includes "empty prefix" {"n1","n1","n1-netns"}. Fine.

Now, are there any real findings? Let me think about the docker test more carefully — the test creates a fake server and DockerRuntime. One issue: `DockerRuntime` has other fields; constructing partially is fine.

Potential flakiness: `httptest.NewServer` — fine.

Also, `nodes/tailscale/proxy.go` LongName: longName — fine.

Now think about whether `LabContainerName` has a subtle flaw when `LongName` ends with `ShortName` but the reference is the node itself or something. Not a new issue.

One potential nitpick: the doc comment says "Only the end of LongName is the short name" — slightly awkward wording but clear.

Another possible finding: the helper name `LabContainerName(shortName string)` — the parameter shadows the concept; callers pass arbitrary net-mode targets which may be external container names. The resulting string is only used as a probe before falling back. Minor.

Is there a case where the new probe name could match a wrong container, causing the external fallback to never trigger? E.g., node in lab "dc1" with network-mode: container:my-external-container. New probe: "clab-dc1-" + "my-external-container" = "clab-dc1-my-external-container". If that container exists in the lab (a node named "my-external-container" — impossible? node names could contain dashes; a node could literally be named "my-external-container"!). Then the reference resolves to the internal node rather than the external container. But that ambiguity is inherent to the design (documented: reference can be internal node name or external name; internal takes precedence). Same as before. Not a finding.

What about a case where the internal probe now matches but didn't before, changing behavior? Old probe was prefix-before-first-occurrence + ref. For the standard case (no name overlap) old == new. For overlap cases, old probe was a garbage name that typically doesn't exist → fell back to external unprefixed ref. Could old fallback have succeeded where new probe now matches? E.g., user has an external container named "srl02-netns" (unprefixed) AND the node's holder is "clab-srl02-srl02-netns". Old: probe "clab-srl02-netns" fails → fallback to external "srl02-netns" → succeeds. New: probe "clab-srl02-srl02-netns" matches the internal holder → uses internal. That is the intended fix (internal takes precedence). Fine.

Now let me also check `GetContainer` name filter: FilterType "name", Match contName, regex `^contName$`. Note the regex anchors: if contName contains regex-special chars, it would fail. Names with dots (e.g., referencing container with dots) — pre-existing.

Now, checks regarding docs: nothing to change. Schema: no change. Tests: added, good.

So findings? This looks like a clean, well-tested bug fix. Possible minor findings:

1. P4: The docker test could also cover the fallback path, but that's pre-existing behavior. Optional. Probably not worth reporting.

2. P4: `LabContainerName` silently assumes `LongName` ends with `ShortName`; when it doesn't (e.g., empty `LongName`), the result is just the passed name. Could document. It's fine as-is given the doc comment explains the end-of-LongName assumption. Maybe skip.

3. Doc: no release note entry for a user-visible bug fix — repo keeps rn per version; current head has 0.82 as latest and there's likely a "dev" version. Let me check what version is in the repo (goreleaser or .github/vars.env, or check `cmd/version`). The fix would land in the next release; rn entries in this repo seem to be added with releases (0.47.md referenced a fix #1676). If the current dev version has no rn file yet, that's normal. I'll put this in coverage notes rather than a finding. Actually, hmm, let me check the version. Let me grep for version in cmd or goreleaser config.

Now let me finalize findings. Do I have any genuine actionable findings?

Candidate findings:

1. P4 — `LabContainerName` relies on the implicit invariant that `LongName` always ends with `ShortName`, but neither the doc comment nor tests state what happens when `LongName` doesn't end with `ShortName` (e.g., `LongName` empty for tool-created configs). If such a config ever reaches `processNetworkMode`/`createContainerSpec`, the result silently degrades to the raw reference name. Given ext-container sets LongName == ShortName and that path is intentional, this is minor. Is it worth reporting? It's a P4 maintainability note. Hmm — the doc comment already explains the end-of-LongName semantics ("Only the end of LongName is the short name"). The behavior when not ending is a graceful fallback that matches old behavior for the ext-container case. I think this is fine and I would not report it — it might be noise.

2. Consider the "node name in prefix" test case: {"lab-x-lab", "lab", "lab-x-lab-netns"} — wait, is that realistic? LongName "lab-x-lab" with ShortName "lab": TrimSuffix("lab-x-lab","lab") = "lab-x-" → "lab-x-lab-netns". But hold on: is that correct? LongName = prefix("lab") + labName("x") + nodeName("lab") → yes "lab-x-lab". TrimSuffix gives "lab-x-" and appends "lab-netns" → "lab-x-lab-netns". Correct.

But there's a subtle failure mode in the reverse direction: what if LongName ends with ShortName but the trailing occurrence is actually part of the lab name, not the node name? That would mean LongName ≠ prefix+lab+"-"+ShortName... i.e., LongName = "clab-<lab>-<node>" where node name doesn't produce the ending... The invariant holds by construction (nodeLongName). Except ext-container (LongName == ShortName → empty prefix, intended) and sros components (suffix appended to both). So the invariant holds everywhere it matters.

Wait — one more: `nodes/default_node_test.go` line 915: LongName "clab-srsim-sros"... test only.

3. What about `PodmanRuntime` external-container fallback? Docker has a fallback to the unprefixed external name; podman does NOT (it always uses the lab-prefixed value). Pre-existing asymmetry; not worsened.

4. Docker test naming/structure: fine.

5. One thing I should double check: the docker test's NodeConfig has no `Labels`; processNetworkMode doesn't need them. OK.

6. Check that the new tests actually pass logically: podman test expects `sg.NetNS.Value == "clab-srl02-srl02-netns"`. In createContainerSpec, for NetworkMode "container:srl02-netns", NSMode "container", Value = LabContainerName("srl02-netns") = TrimSuffix("clab-srl02-srl02","srl02")+"srl02-netns" = "clab-srl02-"+"srl02-netns" = "clab-srl02-srl02-netns". ✓.

But wait — does `createContainerSpec` return before reaching the switch if some earlier code errors with the minimal cfg? Looking at the earlier part of the function (lines 1-140): it processes entrypoint, env, labels, ports, etc. The other tests in the same file use the same minimal config (LongName, ShortName, Image, Labels) and pass presumably, so the new test config is fine. But the new test also omits ExtraHosts — TestCreateContainerSpecAppliesNoneNetworkMode includes ExtraHosts; is HostAdd required non-nil? For container mode, specNetConfig doesn't set HostAdd. Fine.

Hmm, but there's one thing to check in createContainerSpec before the netMode switch: does it call `r.mgmt` or other fields that are nil in the test? mgmt is set. `r.convertPortMap(ctx, cfg.PortBindings)` only in bridge case. OK.

7. Now — a deeper check on the podman side: is there a podman equivalent where the netns holder reference must also be resolved for `srl`/`sros` kinds? The podman path uses the same `container:` network mode, so it's covered.

8. Also check: `processNetworkMode` — for the docker path, after resolving the internal container, they set NetworkMode to "container:<name>". But the netns holder's actual container name is the LongName — correct.

So: genuinely, I find no blocking defects. Are there any worthwhile P4 suggestions?

- One: the podman regression test duplicates naming expectations; fine.
- Two: `LabContainerName` param could be named `name` instead of `shortName` since callers pass net-mode targets that may be external names. Very minor; P4? The doc comment says "returns the long container name of a node in the same lab as n" — calling it with an external name still "probes" the lab-prefixed name. The parameter name `shortName` is accurate for the internal-node use case. I'd skip.

- Three: The docker test asserts only on `hostConfig.NetworkMode`; also `containerConfig.Hostname` should have been unset ("") — could assert that too. Minor. Not worth it.

Hmm, wait. Let me reconsider one potential real issue: in the docker test, the mock server is started before the client; the DockerRuntime `config: clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{Timeout: defaultTimeout}`. ListContainers uses `context.WithTimeout(ctx, d.config.Timeout)`. OK.

Another potential issue: the test at line 28 checks `strings.Contains(r.URL.Query().Get("filters"), "^"+holder+"$")` — the filters JSON contains the regex. OK.

Now, one more angle: is `processNetworkMode`'s first GetContainer now called with a name that could accidentally regex-match another container? The filter is anchored `^name$` so exact match. OK.

Now, final review assembly. Findings assessment:

The change is a focused, correct bug fix with tests. Do I report anything?

Possible findings worth reporting:

A) P4 — `LabContainerName` doc/behavior nuance: When `LongName` does not end with `ShortName` (only meaningful for configs where `LongName == ShortName`, e.g., ext-containers and tool-created configs), the helper returns the raw `shortName` argument, silently degrading to the unprefixed name. That is intentional for ext-containers, but the doc comment doesn't state this fallback. Is this actionable? Marginal. The doc comment says "Only the end of LongName is the short name, as the lab name can contain the same text" — it implicitly covers this. I think this is noise; skip.

B) P4 — The docker regression test exercises only the internal-resolution path; the external-container fallback (`contName = netMode[1]`) is untested. Optional. Marginal — pre-existing path. Skip? The instructions say don't meet a quota; only useful findings. I'll include coverage notes instead.

C) Docs: no doc change needed (behavior now matches documentation). No release note file exists yet for the fix; rn entries appear to be per-release. I'll note in coverage notes that no release-note entry accompanies the fix and whether one is needed is a maintainer convention decision (the in-repo rule `.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc` was listed in the manifest but not available in the snapshot, so I couldn't verify the convention).

Hmm, actually, wait. Should I double check one more potential real issue: the podman `createContainerSpec` path for the srl-netns holder reference — with podman, referencing "container:srl02-netns" where the holder's LongName is "clab-srl02-srl02-netns". Fixed. Good.

One more potential issue with `LabContainerName` semantics vs. the reference being a *container ID*: docker supports `container:<id>`. Old: prefix+id (garbage) → GetContainer fails → fallback to id → works. New: TrimSuffix(...) + id → also garbage (prefix+id) → fails → fallback to id → works. Same.

And if the referenced ID happens to be a 64-hex ID that, with prefix appended, matches some container? Extremely unlikely; same as before anyway.

D) One thing worth double-checking: `types/node_config_test.go` "lab name contains node name" case: {"clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1", "leaf1", "clab-dc1-leaf1-leaf1-netns"}. Correct per TrimSuffix.

E) Is there an issue with `strings.TrimSuffix` when `ShortName` is a suffix of `LongName` but the lab name ends exactly with the ShortName AND the node name is different? E.g., lab "leaf1", node "leaf2": LongName "clab-leaf1-leaf2", ShortName "leaf2". TrimSuffix strips "leaf2" → "clab-leaf1-" + ref. Correct. What if node "leaf1" (same as lab): "clab-leaf1-leaf1" → strip "leaf1" → "clab-leaf1-". Correct — the strip removes only ONE trailing occurrence. Good.

But here's the real edge: lab "leaf1", node "leaf1", and the LongName is "clab-leaf1-leaf1". What if instead the nodeLongName produced "clab-leaf1-leaf1" — yes that's it. Fine.

F) What about `Prefix = "__lab-name"`: LongName = labName + "-" + nodeName. If lab "x" and node "x": LongName "x-x", ShortName "x" → TrimSuffix → "x-" → "x-x-netns". Correct.

G) What if Prefix is custom, e.g. "myprefix": LongName "myprefix-lab-node". Fine.

So the change is sound. Given the thorough check, I'll report a small number of low-severity items or none. Let me think about whether there's anything genuinely worth flagging.
